華泰 (2329 TT)  Trading Buy

獲利逐步增加，建議 Trading Buy 。  
投資建議 ：華泰目前 IC 封測業務佔營收 58%，受惠 NAND Flash 
轉為賣方市場與 AI 儲存需求，封測稼動率回升至約 70%；EMS
業務佔 42%， 主要由佔其營收逾 70% 的美系 AI 伺服器核心客戶
拉貨所帶動。隨全球資料中心算力升級， AI 伺服器出貨量維持
增長， 2Q27 新建廠房正式投產，華泰在 AI 伺服器主板、 eSSD 
一條龍封測與模組代工份額將進一步擴大，推升 2H26 至 2027
全年之營運動能與獲利能見度。預估 2026 年和 2027 年 EPS 分
別為 2.33 元和 3.17 元。華泰因美系大客戶公司的治理與法規合
規爭議大，再加上記憶體封測的評價較低，因此華泰的整體本
益比低於同業。但華泰隨著 2H26 ASP 提升和新產品貢獻，獲利
逐步增加，因此投資為 Trading Buy ，目標價 50.6 元。  
2Q26 稅後 EPS 0.63 元：記憶體客戶備貨積極，半導體營收回
升，佔全公司比重上升，使產能利用率維持高檔。 EMS 事業中
心則受惠 AI 伺服器客戶需求佳。華泰 2Q26 營收 54.76 億元，
QoQ+12.67% ，其中半導體事業部 2Q26 營收達 31.67 億元，
QoQ+16.26% ， 半導體事業產品營收比重分別為 ： 記憶體佔 72%、
邏輯 IC 佔 16% 及測試服務佔 12% 。由於邏輯 IC 有部份是 SSD 
controller ，佔全公司營收比重增加至 58% 。 EMS 部份，營收為  
23.09 億元， QoQ+8.10% ，佔全公司營收比重為 42% 。 2Q26 毛
利率來自半導體事業產能利用率維持高檔 。 2Q26 毛利率 13.25%
較 1Q26 增加 0.46 個百分點， 2Q26 稅後 EPS 0.62 元。  
預估 2026 全年資本支出將超過 25 億元： 華泰過去兩年的年度
資本支出維持在 20 億元左右， 1H26 資本支出已達約 14.77 億
元，加計新建廠房與設備投入，預估 2026 全年資本支出將超過
25 億元。為突破現有廠房產能擁擠瓶頸並因應高階封測需求激
增，華泰於高雄園區建置之全新 9 層樓高先進封裝廠房正全面
進行無塵室機電工程，預計於 2Q27 正式落成並投產。公司採取
審慎務實的階梯式擴充策略，第一階段將優先啟用其中 3 個樓
層建置高等級無塵室，

2 群益投顧 
  華泰 ESG ：  
華泰一直以來所秉持的企業核心價值為「誠正信實、主動創新、異體同心、
積極用心」 ，落實企業誠信及企業道德標準是企業永續治理的關鍵，透過有
效的公司治理架構與董事會職能強化公司治理之作為以不斷創造企業的價
值；除外，公司設有薪酬委員會以保障員工福利並維繫公司競爭力，營運過
程以內控制度確保相關程序之正確性也是營運透明化很重要的基礎。  
環境方面，華泰電子在營運上推行許多節省能資源、減少廢棄物以及妥當處
理廢污水、廢棄物的措施。未來也將持續關注氣候變遷與全球暖化議題，對
於溫室氣體排放進行系統化管理與統計，共同維護自然環境、為地球永續盡
一份心力。  
華泰電子極力追尋為員工創造優質職場環境以及專業能力的培訓。不僅是
人才的培育、員工的身心狀態的健康，期望能創造出幸福的健康職場，展現
華泰人的活力。  
華泰主要業務為 IC 封裝與測試製造服務、與專業電子代工製造服務：  
華泰成立於 06/1971 ，為電子產品製造服務和積體電路封裝測試製造服務供
應商，擁有成品事業中心和半導體事業中心兩大事業部門。半導體事業以
NAND Flash 封裝測試為主；成品事業部則為 EMS 代工，以伺服器為主，
SSD 在近年也成為主力之一，全公司目前 5,800 人。頎邦 (6147 TT) 為最大股
東，持股 35.56% 。  
華泰電子製造服務 (EMS/CEM) 服務項目涵蓋：印刷電路板佈線 (PCB 
Layout) 、製造最佳化設計 (DFM) 、測試最佳化設計 (DFT) 、快速原型組裝
(Prototype) 、印刷電路板組裝量產 (PCB Assembly) 、功能性 /可靠度測試
(Function &Reliability Testing) 、系統組裝 (System integration) 等。積體電路
(IC) 封裝測試服務涵蓋：積體電路 (IC) 及各種半導體零組件封裝測試。公司
自 2003 年開始進行產品線及市場定位調整，已將半導體營運重心聚焦於
NAND Flash 各項應用產品之封裝測試。並引進策略性股東金士頓，也是華
泰 NAND Flash 的主要客戶 ； 而EMS 的主要客戶則為 Supermicro(SMCI US) ，
其佔 EMS 約 70% 營收。  
華泰 2Q26 營收 54.76 億元， QoQ+12.67% ，稅後淨利 4.08 億元，
QoQ+73.97% ，稅後 EPS 0.62 元：  
半導體事業中心受惠記憶體產業成為賣方市場，記憶體客戶備貨積極，半導
體營收回升，佔全公司比重上升，使產能利用率維持高檔。 EMS 事業中心
則受惠 AI 伺服器客戶需求佳，再加上記憶體客戶備貨積極，一條龍生產企
業級  SSD 模組，使 2Q26 營運持續向上。  
華泰 2Q26 營收 54.76 億元 ，QoQ+12.67% ， 其中半導體事業部因 NAND Flash
客戶訂單增加明顯， NAND Flash 價格持續上升，華泰的模組客戶積極擴大
下單。半導體事業部營運展現成長趨勢， 2Q26 營收達 31.67 億元，
QoQ+16.26% ，半導體事業產品營收比重分別為：記憶體佔 72%、邏輯 IC 佔
16% 及測試服務佔 12%。由於邏輯 IC 有部份是 SSD controller ，因此半導體
的封測是以記憶體為主 。 2Q26 半導體事業佔全公司營收比重由 1Q26 的 56% 
增加至 58% 。

### Page 3

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 5 日

3 群益投顧 
2Q26 EMS 部份，主要營收來源以伺服器、工業電腦與消費性電子為主，其
中伺服器相關產品受惠  AI 伺服器與資料中心算力建設強勁拉貨，美系核
心 AI 伺服器客戶訂單佔整體 EMS 營收比重超過 70% ，承製  GPU/CPU 主
機板與周邊加速板 (SMT/PCBA) 維持高速出貨，帶動 EMS 業績穩健攀升。
2Q26 EMS 營收為  23.09 億元， QoQ+8.10% ，佔全公司營收比重為 42% 。  
2Q26 毛利率增加 ， 來自半導體事業產能利用率維持高檔 ， 產能利用率約 70%
和 1Q26 相近，因為產能在持續擴充中，產能利用率提升較慢。 2Q26 毛利
率 13.25% 較 1Q26 增加 0.46 個百分點，但較 2Q25 減少 1.72 個百分點，最
主要是折舊費用增加，和產品組合改變， single die 封測佔比提升 (消費性電
子降低容量，堆疊的量較低 )， 2Q26 稅後淨利 4.08 億元， QoQ+73.97% ，稅
後 EPS 0.62 元。  
營運資金效率同步提升，負債比率由 1Q26 的 49.3% 大幅降至 38.44% ，應
收帳款週轉天數自 1Q26 的 87 天降至 74 天 ， 存貨週轉天數亦由1Q26 的  59 
天改善至 48 天。  
2Q26 股東權利大幅增加，部份來自子公司持有頎邦約 30,320 張，約佔頎邦
持股 4%，1H26 認列於其他綜合損益之金融資產未實現評價利益 (FVOCI) 約
增加 50.5 億元，惟考量該部份為未實現評價利益，且頎邦股價後續仍可波
動，因此 2026 年底股東權益採較保守假設。  
預估 2026 全年資本支出將超過 25 億元：  
華泰過去兩年的年度資本支出維持在 20 億元左右， 2024 年約 19.59 億元、
2025 年達 23.16 億元。 1H26 資本支出已達約 14.77 億元，加計新建廠房與
設備投入，預估 2026 全年資本支出將超過 25 億元。資本支出重點包含高度
自動化晶圓薄化、多晶粒垂直堆疊等高階高容量記憶體封裝設備。建置新廠
之覆晶封裝 (Flip Chip) 與系統級封裝 (SiP) 專用生產線。 2027 年資本支出預
算額度，將待年底董事會審議後定案。  
產能擴充時程與硬體資本支出規劃方面，為突破現有廠房產能擁擠瓶頸並
因應高階封測需求激增，華泰於高雄園區建置之全新 9 層樓高先進封裝廠
房正全面進行無塵室機電工程，預計於 2Q27 正式落成並投產。該新廠總樓
地板面積約達 7 萬平方公尺，公司採取審慎務實的階梯式擴充策略，第一階
段將優先啟用其中 3 個樓層建置高等級無塵室，主力配置覆晶封裝 (Flip 
Chip) 及系統級封裝 (SiP) 全自動化產線，並承接自企業總部騰挪出之覆晶產
能，騰出之總部空間則可彈性擴充傳統打線封裝機台 (Wire Bonder) ，以承接
一線封測大廠產能挪移至先進封裝所釋出之成熟邏輯  IC 外溢訂單。  
2Q26 毛利率較 2Q25 同期略為承壓，主因之一即為設備與新廠投資所衍生。
2024 年約 8.34 億元、 2025 年達 9.75 億元。 1H26 約 5.55 億元。 9 層樓新廠
採取分樓層逐步啟用的模式，正是為避免一次性提列過高折舊成本，以維護
資本支出與折舊費用的健康平衡。  
預估華泰 2026 年營收 215.74 億元， YoY+9.61% ，稅後獲利 15.34 億元，
YoY+8.77% ，稅後 EPS 2.33 元：  
先前華泰半導體封測與 EMS 代工業務營收比重約各為 60% ～ 70% 與 30% ～
40%，整體獲利動能與半導體封測景氣循環緊密連動。以往封測代工代價談

### Page 4

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 5 日

4 群益投顧 
判為後段產業常態，平均銷售單價 (ASP) 具競爭壓力，惟隨 AI 帶動封測需求
增加，大廠訂單外溢，高階儲存需求激增與原廠比重持續拉升，機台稼動率
提升對毛利率之正向營運槓桿效益顯著；加上公司已於 2H26 針對部分緊俏
封測產能微幅調漲價格，有助獲利結構持續優化。  
華泰半導體事業中心因應邊緣裝置與高階資料傳輸對巨量頻寬與極致縮裝
的需求，全面推進次世代封裝技術架構升級。公司目前主力發展之複合鍵合
/混合封裝， Hybrid Bonding ，整合打線 Wire Bond 與覆晶 Flip Chip 之多晶
粒 SiP 系統級封裝技術 )，已成功量產並出貨應用於  micro SD 7.0 、eUFS 4.x
及 uSSD 等高階記憶體產品，將記憶體晶粒 (Memory Die) 透過打線堆疊、主
控晶片 (Controller) 透過覆晶技術共同整合封裝於單一模組內 ， 作為未來高附
加價值產品線之核心成長動能。在次世代先進封裝中長期研發方面，華泰亦
深化與核心客戶合作，積極開發扇出型覆晶晶圓級封裝 (Fan-Out FCCSP) 等
高階架構，預計於 4Q26 至 2027 年逐步推進至工程試產階段，卡位高速邊
緣運算與客製化 ASIC 儲存市場。  
目前已有 2 家原廠進入華泰前十大客戶名單，並陸續有新原廠接洽驗證。原
廠級訂單具備量體大、能見度長之特性。低容量 eMMC EOL 紅利，受原廠
產能轉移至高階  AI 儲存影響，成熟低容量 eMMC 陸續進入停產 (EOL) 階
段，缺口由台灣及區域晶片商填補，帶動國內記憶體客戶下單積極。  
EMS 事業中心則維持高速成長動能，伺服器主機板 (Server PCBA) 與核心客
戶維持長年深度合作，伺服器相關訂單佔 EMS 營收比重達 70% ，其中美系
AI 伺服器核心大客戶 Supermicro 佔整體伺服器營收比重逾 90% ；由於
Supermicro 訂單模式以帶料生產為主 ， 其營收規模與獲利貢獻顯著高於未帶
料之純加工模組。隨全球資料中心算力升級引領  AI 伺服器出貨量維持增
長，2Q27 新建廠房正式投產，華泰在  AI 伺服器主板、 eSSD 一條龍封測與
模組代工份額將進一步擴大，推升 2H26 至 2027 全年之營運動能與獲利能
見度。預估 2026 年和 2027 年 EPS 分別為 2.33 元和 3.17 元。

預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

1 
台積電可能與馬斯克合作興建並 
營運「Terafab」評析

報告日期： 2025/09/19 研 究 員：羅  金 鳳 (Sunny Lo)  
新 聞 評 析 
新聞重點摘要： 
台積電可能與馬斯克合作興建並營運「 Terafab 」。

一. 我們認為成案可能性極低  
我們認為台積電僅基於商業友好關係與馬斯克進行商討，但成案
的可能性極低，主因台積電表明過其晶圓廠的建置與營運都以
100% 自主擁有為主 。 雖然目前在德國的德勒斯登的ESMC 為合資
(台積電持股 70% 、博世持股 10% 、英飛凌持股 10% 、恩智浦持
股 10%) ，及在日本的熊本廠 JASM 為合資 (台積電持股 86.5% 、
Sony 持股  6.0% 、DENSO 持股 5.5% 、Toyota 持股 2.0%) ，但大
都因 2021 ～2022 年 COVID-19 期間車用晶圓持續供不應求，廠
商為了避免因半導體產能供貨不足而影響出貨的情況發生，紛紛
以能確保半導體晶圓供充足為優先，且後續接續 AI 需求蓬勃發
展，晶圓需求緊俏程度更加嚴重，各國紛紛力邀台積電到該國設
廠，日本及德國為汽車製造大國，因此，台積電於日本及德國的
設廠大都以車用的成熟製程或特殊製程為主 (僅 Sony 以 CMOS 影
像感測為主 )，在當地政府的建廠補貼下，也開放該國企業持有部
份股權。

我們認為本次傳言台積電可能與馬斯克合作興建並營運
「 Terafab 」的技術層次將會拉高到最先進製程的水準，一旦
Terafab 開始營運後，將直接與台積電的先進製程產生衝突，因
此，我們認為成案的可能性極低。馬斯克建置的晶圓廠初期的想
法也是因為晶圓代工產能持續供不應求，馬斯克將建置自有的晶
圓廠滿足旗下 Tesla 車用晶片 、Optimus 機器人 、Dojo 訓練晶片、
AI 晶片、低軌衛星晶片，尤其因目前 AI 的競爭發展白熱化，晶片
的運算能力及設計複雜度都大幅提升，使用最先進的製程節點勢
在必行，因此，馬斯克先前也曾於 2026 年 4 月宣布將與 Intel 合
作 14A 製程，導入 Terafab 進行生產製造，由於 14A 將是 Intel
下一階段最先進的製程，顯見馬斯克對最先進的製程有強烈的需
求，其 Terafab 勢必以發展最先進製程為主，因此，若台積電與
馬斯克合作，勢必也要以其最先進的製程 2nm 為主，甚至下一世
代的 16A ，才能滿足馬斯克的期待，如此，台積電先進製程機密
可能有外洩風險，且若如馬斯克宣稱的 Terafab 初期產能 10 萬片

下的主要受惠者，先進製程、先進封裝同步大幅優於競爭對手，

Document 093: 7856 Hermes TP8800 UBS 20261008

Date: 2026-10-08

### Page 1

ab 8 October 2026
Global Research
Initiation of Coverage
Hermes Testing Solutions
Shifting-left, scaling-up: dual catalysts in chip
probing and CPO
Jack-of-all-trades for bespoke testing solutions in the semiconductor industry
We initiate coverage of Hermes Testing (HTSI) with a Buy rating and NT$8,880 PT. HTSI is
uniquely positioned to benefit from two key semiconductor testing trends: shift-left
testing and the emerging CPO testing market. Leveraging its long-standing relationship

with TSMC, HTSI has established capabilities across electrical test engineering,
customised test equipment, thermal control modules, probe cards, and CPO testing
solutions. We forecast revenue growth of 127%/152%/78% in 2026E/27E/28E, driven
by increasing adoption of thermal control modules for wafer-level test and initial
deployment of CPO insertion 2 and insertion 3 test equipment through ficonTEC.
Additional 2028E growth drivers include reflow tools, laser clean solutions, and MEMS

probe cards. The stock trades at 27/15x 2027/28E PE, versus ~35x NTM PE for handler-
equipment peers, offering re-rating potential as sales scale, new products are qualified,
and its CPO presence expands. See our companion testing deep dive sector note.
Shift-left beneficiary with exceptional thermal control modules
Thermal control modules should be HTSI's largest growth driver over the next several
years. We expect increasing chip power consumption, rising package complexity, and

tighter probe pitches to drive adoption of advanced thermal control solutions used
alongside leading-edge probers, in additional to more wafer level insertions and longer
test time. We estimate HTSI will ship approximately 3,000 thermal-control modules in
2027E, up from around 1,200 units in 2026E, sales contribution rise from ~30% in
Q226 to >50% of sales by 2027E. The launch of its second-generation platform in
2027E, which carries 25-50% higher ASPs, should further improve profitability. We

forecast GM expansion from 55.5% in 2026E to 58.3%/60.0% in 2027E/28E.
Unique position to capture CPO upside
Through its partnership with German optical-automation leader ficonTEC, HTSI is
positioned to participate in the initial ramp of CPO testing equipment. HTSI supports
local manufacturing, installation, automation and after-sales service, making it a key
enabler of volume production. We estimate HTSI captures approximately 30% of
equipment value across the 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems we forecast for

delivery in 2027E, with further potential upside in 2028E as adoption expands.
Valuation: initiate coverage with a Buy rating and price target NT$8,880
We forecast EPS of NT$29.1 in 2026E (including a one-off employee share purchase
programme impact), rising to NT$153.3/286.5 in 2027E/28E. Our NT$8,880 price target
is based on 31x average 2028E PE, versus handler-equipment peers forward PE at
roughly 35x, reflecting HTSI's unique exposure to both shift-left testing, CPO, and our

estimate of 32% LT earnings CAGR. We initiate with a Buy rating.
Highlights (NT$m) 12/24 12/25 12/26E 12/27E 12/28E 12/29E 12/30E

Thesis Map     UBS                    Research     THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Will HTSI be able to keep its moat within chip probing thermal control modules?
Yes. We expect HTSI's competitive position to strengthen as chip power consumption, package
complexity, and probe density continue to increase. The company is developing next-generation

thermal control solutions, including liquid cooling technologies, while also expanding into specialized
chuck solutions designed to address wafer warpage. In addition, HTSI's close collaboration with
TSMC, TEL, Advantest, and other OSATs should reinforce its position within advanced wafer-level
testing.
Q: Will insertion 2 of CPO testing process be skipped?
No. We believe concerns surrounding insertion 2 adoption are overdone. While some industry

participants favor bypassing insertion 2 due to throughput and ROI considerations, doing so increases
the risk of defective optical engines being identified only after the components are delivered to
OSATs, creating yield accountability challenges and higher packaging losses. Based on our industry
studies, we expect insertion 2 testing to remain an important part of the CPO workflow over at least
the next two years as the industry focuses on improving PIC yields and overall testing efficiency.

We initiate coverage of Hermes Testing (HTSI) with a Buy rating and NT$8,880 price target. HTSI
offers unique exposure to two of the semiconductor testing industry's most attractive growth drivers:
1) shift-left testing and 2) CPO testing. The industry's shift-left testing strategy will have more test
insertions move to the wafer level before packaging, in addition to longer CP time. Leveraging its
longstanding relationship with TSMC, HTSI has established capabilities across thermal control

modules, electrical-test engineering services, customized equipment, and emerging CPO testing
solutions. We believe there could be re-rating potential as sales scale, new products are qualified, and
its CPO presence expands.
TSMC and OSATs have been expanding their CoWoS capacities aggressively, from 40kwpm in 2024
to around 90 kwpm in 2025, and is tracking to expand CoWoS capacity toward 160kwpm in 2026E.
TSMC also ramped up expanding N3/N2 capacity, with multiple product launches from customers'

pipelines—Nvidia's Rubin, AMD's MI400 series/Venice CPU and other cloud ASICs—translating into
foreseeable, strong testing demand.
Despite a 264% YTD share-price increase, HTSI currently trades at just 15x of our 2028E EPS versus
handler-equipment peers forward PE at roughly 35x. We believe market expectations for 2027 CPO
testing equipment shipments remain conservative, particularly for insertion 2 equipment. Based on
our industry analysis, we estimate HTSI will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E,

this should be higher than the market's expectation of no insertion 2 and limited insertion 3 shipment
due to engineering bottleneck. We also see further upside in 2028E as CPO adoption expands. Our
reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market price implies only a 17% long-term sales CAGR
vs our estimate of 27% and long-term GM level of 33% vs our estimate of 61%. We see scope for
re-rating.

Testing TAM expanding with IC complexity: leaders likely to
outperform industry growth
Our central view is that testing content per AI accelerator is growing faster than
semiconductor unit volumes, creating a multi-year expansion in testing TAM. We believe
semiconductor testing is entering a period of structural growth as AI-driven chip
complexity increases testing intensity across wafer, package and system levels. The

industry's shift-left strategy, which moves more testing upstream in the semiconductor
manufacturing process, is resulting in longer and more frequent test insertions. As cloud
AI GPUs and ASICs become increasingly complex, additional testing is required to ensure
functionality, performance and yield, while reducing defective parts per million (DPPM).
More robust testing should improve deployment efficiency and lower the cost of
operating AI infrastructure.

Semiconductor testing generally comprises four stages: wafer/chip probing (CP), final
test (FT), system-level test (SLT), and burn-in. CP is conducted at the wafer-level before
packaging, while FT, SLT, and burn-in occur after packaging. The below Figure 3S e m i c o n d u c t o r  t e s t i n g  p r o c e s s :  f r o m  w a f e r  l e v e l  t o  p a c k a g e  l e v e l
illustrates the semiconductor manufacturing flow.

We estimate semiconductor testing historically accounts for ~1% of total
semiconductor revenue. Within testing, consumables such as probe cards, sockets and
interface boards represent ~10-15% of industry revenue. According to Yole, the probe
card, socket and interface board markets should grow from US$3.8bn, US$2.3bn and
US$1.7bn in 2026 to US$5.5bn, US$3.1bn and US$2.0bn by 2031, respectively.

We see HTSI's sales for the next few years to mainly be driven by 1) thermal control
module shipment and 2) CPO equipment shipment. In the past, thermal control

modules weren't necessary for chip probing, due to less TDP for the chips. However, as
new iterations of AI accelerators now got substantial power draw, this unlocks a brand
new TAM for HTSI to enter with their thermal control modules to pair with probers, in
order to prevent probe card damages.
 Robust thermal control module shipments.  We estimate HTSI to ship
3,000/4,100 thermal control modules in 2027E/28E, with continuous demand in
future years, bringing their 2026-31E semiconductor equipment sales CAGR to

48%.

Robust growth drivers across semiconductor testing
We believe HTSI is set to benefit from several semiconductor testing growth drivers and
capture the AI induced, expanding testing TAM. We see HTSI is uniquely positioned as
the main beneficiary of two of the strongest growth drivers within semiconductor
testing: testing shift-left and CPO.
Shift-left igniting secular growth for chip probing

### Page 5

Hermes Testing Solutions 8 October 2026 ab 5
The industry's adoption of shift-left testing should drive a multi-year structural growth
opportunity for chip probing (CP) and its supply chain. As advanced packaging costs rise
and AI accelerators become increasingly complex, semiconductor manufacturers are
placing greater emphasis on identifying known-good dies (KGD) before packaging.
Figure 4: CP is set to grow substantially with shift-left, each packaging step set to be MORE costly than the prior step

RDL / RWAT testing Wafer-level CP (WLCP)
CoWChiplet (if use SoIC) CoW
CP (Die A)
CP (Die B)
Assembly Test (AST)
oS
Source: UBS
The economics of advanced packaging reinforce this trend. Heterogeneous integration,
chiplet architectures and 2.5D/3D packaging significantly increase package value, while
defects identified late in the manufacturing process can lead to substantial scrap costs.
To mitigate this risk, foundries and OSATs such as TSMC and ASE employ distributed

testing flows incorporating KGD, known-good-stack (KGS) and assembly test screening,
to catch defects before the components enter a multi-week packaging cycle (>2 weeks
for CoW and 2 weeks for oS). The importance of these tests rises as AI clusters become
increasingly dependent on component reliability and low defective-parts-per-million
(DPPM) rates. Given the multiplicative yield degradation, a 300bps yield decline at just
one manufacturing stage (e.g. dropping from 99.5% to 96.5%) reduces overall

integrated four-stage yield from 98.0% to 95.0%; on a 500,000 unit ramp at US
$10,000 per unit, this 3% scrap delta translates into around US$150m in unrecoverable
losses given >6-month manufacturing lead times.
At the same time, rising chip power consumption is increasing the technical difficulty of
wafer-level testing. As chip thermal design power scales beyond 1,000W and power
density is expected by the industry to surge another 8x by 2030, traditional wafer-level

chip probe (WLCP) encounters severe physical bottlenecks. Finer probe pitches and
higher power densities increase the risk of overheating, probe needle burnout and yield
degradation, creating demand for more advanced probing and thermal-control
solutions.

Hermes Testing (HTSI) is a key beneficiary of this trend. Leveraging its long-standing
relationship with TSMC and expertise in semiconductor test solutions, HTSI supplies

advanced thermal control modules used alongside leading-edge probers (made by
Tokyo Electron), particularly for AI applications. These thermal control modules are
required for high TDP semiconductor wafer probe in order to maintain the temperature

### Page 6

Hermes Testing Solutions 8 October 2026 ab 6
of the chuck to avoid needle burns or probe card damages. We estimate HTSI will ship
approximately 3,000 thermal-management modules in 2027E, up from 1,200 in 2026E,
supporting sales growth of 127%/152% in 2026E/27E. HTSI is also the partner for
prober installation, prober maintenance and services. In addition, HTSI provides high-
end chuck processing services, an area that should benefit from increasing package sizes

and higher wafer warpage associated with advanced packaging.
Beyond thermal control modules, HTSI also supplies consumables and customised
solutions for wafer-level testing, including probe card cleaning sheets and laser-based
probe head cleaning systems. As probing intensity increases under the shift-left testing
model, demand for cleaning and maintenance solutions should rise alongside probe
card utilisation. Currently, cleaning sheets is around 5% of sales, but we believe it could

gain traction in 2027/28E and beyond. We see additional upside from future modular
laser cleaning systems that could be integrated directly with probers, further expanding
HTSI's content opportunity within advanced wafer-level testing.
CPO testing fuelling future growth for semiconductor testing
As interconnect bottlenecks increasingly shift from the memory wall to the copper wall,
we believe co-packaged optics (CPO) adoption is inevitable over the longer term. Testing

remains one of the key bottlenecks to CPO deployment, as optical validation differs
fundamentally from the electrical testing processes that have dominated semiconductor
manufacturing for decades. As a result, CPO is creating a new testing opportunity across
the semiconductor equipment and consumables ecosystem. We have written
extensively on CPO and opportunities that it brings to the semiconductor supply chain,
see our CPO deep dive , our thoughts on CPO timing , 2026 SiPhIA conference

takeaways, and SEMICON 2026 takeaways.
Various test equipment suppliers are scrambling to come up with ideal testing
equipment across the four insertions of CPO testing, with solutions to ensure both
optical and electrical signal integrity of the CPO solutions. The four insertions are:
 Insertion 1: Electronic IC (EIC)/Photonic IC (PIC) test.  Single-side wafer test
that has dedicated probe cards to test the fabricated EIC/PIC wafers.
 Insertion 2: Optical/Electrical test. Double sided wafer test, testing the hybrid

bonded (e.g. via TSMC's COUPE) EIC and PIC wafers by an opto-electrical test that
verifies the electrical signals on the top and optical signal on the bottom of the
hybrid bonded wafers.
 Insertion 3: Optical engine (OE) test.  The OE tests are done to ensure the
quality of the OEs and with FAU (Fiber Array Unit) attached, to ensure the signal
integrity of the OEs. The testing equipment needs to be able to handle 224G per
channel data rates, requiring high unit per hour (UPH) throughput, meticulous dirt

mitigation, and maintaining a near 100% contact rating on high-speed sockets.
 Insertion 4: ASIC final test.  This final test step is established post the OEs are
packaged together with the ASIC. The complete ASIC package (now with CPO) is
now tested for opto-electrical functionalities.
As it stands, insertion 2 and insertion 3 are the two areas where there are the most
debates in the industry. Insertion 3 suppliers such as Chroma has noted the trend of

“shift-to-middle” paradigm centered on insertion 3. In high-density scale-out switches
deploying up to 32 optical engines (e.g. Nvidia’s Spectrum-X architectures), any OE
defect results in package scrap at the CoWoS level, making 100% known good die
(KGD) validation at the singulated OE stage an absolute commercial requirement.

With its 25 years of precision test equipment expertise, ficonTEC is an early mover for
CPO test equipment and has key know-how for optical alignment, which is
quintessential for optical test. HTSI, through its partnership with ficonTEC, is set to be a
major beneficiary once CPO proliferates in 2027E-28E. As the partner of ficonTEC,
Hermes Testing is the key enabler that can help ficonTEC achieve HVM for its CPO

testing equipment. As of now, our view is that both insertion 2 and insertion 3 should be
required within the next two years, despite the difficulties surrounding double-sided
wafer probing (insertion 2), as the yield for OEs are still not high enough to avoid
defective dies. Finding out known-good OEs will be crucial before the FAU coupling
process during the latter stages of CPO packaging, to prevent impeding the final
package with multiple OEs attached to the switch ASIC (e.g. 32 OEs on Nvidia's

Spectrum-X). We estimate Hermes Testing will ship 40/70 units of insertion 2/3 testing
equipment in 2027E, with potential upside to the numbers as we view Hermes Testing
will likely assist with the ramp up for Gen 2 insertion 3 equipment in 2027E. Gen 2
insertion equipment will come with better throughput and ASP .

Beyond insertions 2 and 3, HTSI is also positioned to participate in insertion 1 testing
through customised prober solutions for photonic integrated circuit (PIC) testing. We
believe these solutions could eventually be packaged as licensable intellectual property,

creating an additional long-term growth opportunity as CPO adoption broadens. While
near-term testing growth is driven by AI accelerators, CPO represents the next major
expansion cycle for semiconductor testing. We believe HTSI is uniquely positioned to
participate across multiple test insertions, providing exposure to a potentially significant
source of incremental revenue beginning in 2027E.

HTSI's unique position for new TAM of thermal control
modules and CPO test equipment
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu, Taiwan, as a spin-off
from Hermes-Epitek. The company provides semiconductor testing and inspection

solutions, engineering services, customized equipment, and test consumables. HTSI
maintains a global service footprint across Taiwan (Hsinchu, Tainan), Mainland China
(Suzhou), Japan (Tokyo, Kumamoto), Singapore, Malaysia, and the United States
(Arizona), enabling close support for leading semiconductor customers.
Its main business segments are structured around three pillars:
 Semiconductor equipment and customized products : HTSI utilises a dual-

track strategy of independent R&D and also equipment agency sales. They offers

### Page 9

Hermes Testing Solutions 8 October 2026 ab 9
highly customized solutions designed to address specific client pain points and
bottlenecks. Key products include: thermal control modules that pair with a 1:1
ratio with wafer-level test probers, electron beam inspection equipment, white
light interference optical inspection systems, laser probe cleaning machines, and
the SYMPHONY fluxless reflow system.
 Testing equipment engineering services:  The company operates the largest

engineering service team in Taiwan, consisting of over 340 technical professionals.
The engineering service team provides extensive equipment life-cycle support,
including installation, relocations, modification, testing, routine maintenance, and
repair services. CPO related equipment sales/services will be recognized within this
segment, as CPO are not direct HTSI products, but rather sales recognition via its
partnership with ficonTEC.
 Probe card design, manufacturing, and cleaning materials:  HTSI delivers a

comprehensive product portfolio that supports the entire probe card life-cycle. For
probe cards, they supply membrane, cantilever, and vertical probe cards. As they
are small in scale, HTSI targets niche probe card applications such as RF, 5G/6G,
millimeter wave, LCD driver, low end logics, TIA (Transimpedance Amplifier), etc. In
addition to the three existing probe technologies, HTSI also collaborates with MJC
in Japan for MEMS probe development, targeting sales in 2028E for high-end, AI/

HPC related applications. Cleaning materials is another important business within
this segment, as HTSI source and process sand paper or other materials to offer
cleaning sheets to clean the probe cards, removing oxidations, unwanted particles
and substances that accumulated on the probe cards during the testing process.
The sales resiliency in the coming years mainly stems from its robust relationship with key
industry players, especially as an engineering and service provider with TSMC for testing

solutions. This sets up a solid foundation for its customized equipment business, as they
know the specific testing bottleneck to tackle. For instance, as Hermes-Epitek
manufacturers the probers for Tokyo Electron (TEL) and HTSI helps with installation,
services, and maintenance of the probers, they understand the thermal bottlenecks that
the wafer-level test industry has been facing and can effectively come up with module
solutions to solve this.

Margins set to expand with value-added products and services
The company has demonstrated strong execution in advanced wafer-level testing, with
H126 revenue of NT$2.2bn already exceeding 90% of FY25 revenue (NT$2.4bn).
Profitability has also improved significantly, with GM increasing from 42.1% in 2025 to
54.9% in H126.
With share of value-added products and services set to rise in 2027E in sales mix, HTSI is
set to see GM and operating leverage expansion. As a niche engineering service provider

that has robust ties to leading foundries and OSATs, we believe HTSI has substantial
pricing power and estimate HTSI' GM for thermal control modules to be around 65%,
with GM for CPO equipment of around 60% as HTSI provides an engineering-service-
like business for it. We believe these factors will help the company to reach a 60% long-
term GM range.
OPM is also set to expand with customized equipment sales rise in total sales mix. If we
exclude the one-time expense to be recognized during Q326 for employee stock

benefits (we estimate around NT$1bn), YoY opex growth will be around 99% for
2027E, with downside risk if the company conducts tight cost control measures. We
forecast the company's OPM will expand from 19.5% in 2026E, to 44.3%/47.0% in
2027/28E.

Hon Precision's forward PE Chroma's forward PE Cohu's forward PE

Peer Average = 35x -1std = 18x
Source: LSEG, UBS
Despite a 264% YTD share-price increase, HTSI still trades at just 15x 2028E P/E. We
believe market expectations for 2027E CPO testing equipment shipments remain
conservative, particularly for insertion 2 equipment. Based on our industry analysis, we
estimate HTSI will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E, this should
be higher than the market's expectation of no insertion 2 and limited insertion 3

shipment due to engineering bottleneck. We also see further upside in 2028E as CPO
adoption expands. Our reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market price
implies only a 17% long-term sales CAGR vs our estimate of 27% and long-term GM
level of 33% vs our estimate of 61%. We see scope for re-rating.
We believe the market is not fully reflecting three key growth drivers:
1) Shift-left testing is still underappreciated.
As advanced packaging costs rise and AI accelerators become increasingly complex,

more thorough testing is done at the wafer-level. This should drive sustained demand
for HTSI's thermal control modules, probe card consumables, and other wafer-level
testing solutions.
2) CPO revenue potential remains underestimated.
Investor expectations for 2027 CPO equipment shipments, particularly insertion 2
testing systems, remain conservative. Based on our industry studies, we forecast HTSI
will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E, with further potential

upside in 2028E as CPO adoption broadens.
3) New products provide additional optionality.
Beyond its core businesses, HTSI is expanding into higher-value solutions including mass-
reflow tools, MEMS probe cards and laser cleaning systems, creating incremental
growth opportunities that we believe are largely absent from current expectations.
We value HTSI using 31x average 2028E PE, underpinned by a 32% 2027-31E earnings
CAGR. We use 2028E PE as our view is that 2028 is the year for thermal control modules

and CPO adoption grow from the initial ramping up stage to mass adoption stage. We
believe using handlers as a peer group is justifiable for a unique company like HTSI, with
thermal control modules for chip probing sharing similar characteristics as active thermal
control used for final test, in addition to Hon Precision and Chroma having exposure to
CPO test equipment. For equipment vendors, the peer group's average NTM PE post
2022 is around 35x, and the group is currently trading at around 41x NTM PE (see

What's Priced In chart).
HTSI is trading at NT$4,190 as of 6
Oct 2026

Source: UBS estimates
Risk to the current share price is heavily skewed (2.2:1) to the upside.
UPSIDE (NT$10,000): Our upside scenario reflects stronger than expected thermal
module and CPO equipment shipment in 2027-28E, with additional upside from better
traction of probe cards and mass reflow tools, forming 157%/91% YoY sales growth in
2027/28E and even higher operating leverage with OPM of 46.3%/50.0% in 2027/28E.

This scenario implies a 34x 2028E PE, a upside driving by more equipment demand
stemming for robust shift-left test induced equipment bottleneck, alongside CPO
proliferation with scale-up CPO equipment pull-in in 2028E.
BASE (NT$8,880): Our price target of NT$8,880 is based on 31x 2028E PE. We expect an
earnings CAGR of 32% in 2027-31E. With shift-left trend for more thermal module
requirement during testing, alongside 40/70 CPO insertion 2/3 shipment. We see these

growth drivers driving YoY sales growth from a high base of 127% in 2026E to
152%/78% YoY in 2027/2028E, with OPM of 44.3%/47.0% in 2027/28E.
DOWNSIDE (NT$1,500): Our downside scenario reflects lower shipment of thermal
control modules due to less advanced prober demand with less insertion 2 and 3
equipment shipment due to bottlenecks with CPO production, alongside limited
additional growth drivers to be picked up in 2028 such as MEMS probe card and mass

reflow tools. This leads to weaker YoY sales growth of 67%/50% in 2027/28E and
operating margin of 29.3%/32.0% in 2027/28E as equipment shipment fails to scale.
This scenario implies a 14x 2028E PE, as valuation de-rate with dimmed outlook without
CPO and limited upside from thermal control module shipments.

Company Description
Company Description
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu,
Taiwan. It focuses on providing semiconductor testing and
inspecting solutions, equipment installation, technical support,
and maintenance services. Its main business segments are
customized semiconductor equipment, engineering services, and
probe cards and other materials.

Industry Outlook
New iterations of semiconductors, especially for AI / HPC
applications, are set to increase in both front-end wafer
fabrication advancements and back-end packaging complexities
with each iterations. With the industry now moves to utilize
"shift left", chip probing and its relevant supply chain will be key
beneficiaries of this trend which we believe will unlock multi-
year, secular growth trends.

Company Description
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu, Taiwan. It focuses on
providing semiconductor testing and inspecting solutions, equipment installation, technical
support, and maintenance services. Its main business segments are customized

semiconductor equipment, engineering services, and probe cards and other materials.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Hermes Testing based on PE methodology.
Risks for the semis industry:
- Geopolitical uncertainties: Geopolitical uncertainties including tariffs and more export
control could negatively impact semis cycle, and a downturn would lead to slower demand in
the overall market and moderating capacity expansion.
- Meaningful deceleration of new technology development: Slower demand and less

investment are expected If there is meaningful deceleration of new technology development
such as Cloud AI, autonomous driving, etc.
- Rising utility cost: Larger-than-expected utility cost increases would further pressure
profitability. Rising utility costs in Taiwan have negatively impacted margins for Taiwan tech
companies.
Company risks:
- Faster- or slower-than-expected front-end/CoWoS capacity expansion: This could result in
Hermes Testing gaining/losing equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs.

- Competitors achieving technological advancement: Potential risk of competition (from
either local or foreign companies) achieving technological advancements could result in
Hermes Testing losing market share from major foundries and OSATs.
- Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth: Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth could
lead to a ramp-up or a slowdown of equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs to
Hermes Testing, affecting long-term revenue growth potential.

- Slowdown or delay of new technological advancement: A potential slowdown or delay of
the rollout for technological advancements for technologies such as SoIC, CoPoS, and CPO,
would limit future equipment orders.
- Increase of raw material costs: Rising raw material costs may directly impact Hermes
Testing’s margins for equipment shipments.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

주가 추이

3Q 큰 폭의 판매 감소, 매출/영업이익 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영 업 이 익 은 각각 44.6조원(-4.5% 이하 Y oY)과 2.4조원
(-5.4%) 기록하며 시장 기대치 하회 전망. 3Q 매출액은 글로벌 판매 부진 지속
과 환율 하락, 그리고 친환경차 판매 성장 둔화 등의 영향에 기인. 특히, 3Q 판
매량은 918.7만대(-11.5% 이하 Y oY)로 전년 동기 대비 큰 폭 감소하며 매출
액에 부정적 영향. 이는 7월과 8월 발생한 파업 영향으로 약 5.5만대 규모의 생
산 물량 차질이 발 생 한 부분에 기인. 파업은 기존 물량/믹스 개선에 기 여 했 던 
글로벌 HEV 판매도 둔화(+3.4%)시키면서 매출에 부정적 영향을 심화. 
3Q 수 익 성 은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입에 따른 원가 상승 영향과 친환경차 
판매 감소에 따른 부정적 믹스 영향 불 가 피. 이에 시장 기대치인 3.0조원을 크
게 하회할 전망. 다만, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮아지면서 판매보증비 
외화환산손익 영향은 다소 개선(0.4%p) 예상되며, 관세 영향 또한 전년 동기 
대비 약 1조원(2.1%p) 감소하면서 수익성 악화 영향을 일부 만 회 할 전망.  
’27년 지연된 신차효과 가시화로 반등 여력 충분 
4Q부터는 3Q 파업 영향 등으로 지연된 신차 확대 본격화되고, 현지 수요 높은 
차종 중심의 물량 확대를 통해 매출 회복 및 이익 성장 가시화가 기대. 아 반 떼
와 투싼 FMC 확대로 국내 및 해외 판매 둔화가 점진적 만회되고, 지난 9월부
터 터키에서 아이오닉3 생산이 본격화되면서 유럽에서의 시장 부진 회복이 기
대. 또한, GV80과 G80 HEV 모델 출시로 내년부터 국내/북미 중심 판매 확대
되며 올해 부진했던 물량/믹스 개선에 기여할 것으로 기대. ’27년 HMGMA에
서 HEV 신차 생산 투입 예정되어 있어 상 대 적 으 로 높은 수요가 지속되는 북미
에서의 HEV 현지 생산 확대로 가동률 증 가 와 관세 부담 완화도 수익성 개선에 
기여할 것으로 기대. 
부정적 환율 고려 필요하나 ’27년 강한 증익 기대  
’27년 동사 영 업 이 익 추 정 치 는 12.4조원(+22.3%)으로 ’26년 감 익 에 서 ’27년 
증익 전환 예상. 최근 환율 추세 고려 시 내년 상 반 기 까 지 부정적인 환율 영향 
확대 가 능 성 존재하나, 기존 노후 차 종 에 서 현지 수요 대응 가능한 신차 중심 
생산/판매 확대로 물량/믹스 개선되며 부 정 적 환율 영향 상쇄 기대.  
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-11.8%)과 상반기 적 용 한 코 스 피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)에 따라 목 표 주 가 를 670,000원으로 하향. 그러나, 연 내 RMAC 운영 
성 과 와 RA 설립, 12 월 모셔널 자 율 주 행 상용화와 웨 이 모向 로 보 택 시 
양산/공급 등 미래 사 업 에 서 의 가 시 성 도 여전히 기대 가능한 부분.

자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 8

현대차 (005380) [한화리서치]

8 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

Document 165: Cliff Notes_ the importance of supply (2026-10-13)

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: the importance of supply
By Elliot Clarke, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
09:47 September 25 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s only data release this week was the August Labour Force Survey. Employment surprised to
the upside, rising 39.5k. But, once again, new labour demand was more than offset by supply as the
participation rate bounced 0.2ppts higher to 67.1%, just shy of January 2025’s 115-year high of 67.2%.

As a result, the unemployment rate increased from 4.5% to 4.6% and was close to rounding up to 4.7%
(4.65% to two decimal places).

### Page 2

There may be some residual seasonality in August’s print related to the new survey collection model.
However, the underlying trend is still consistent with a gradual easing in the labour market, seeing the
unemployment rate drift higher. We expect this to continue through to the first half of 2027.
The strength in labour force participation also speaks to the broader issue of Australia’s supply
capacity. As discussed by Chief Economist Luci Ellis, the RBA’s assumptions around participation,

productivity and the NAIRU collectively skew towards a weak assessment of supply capacity. For
participation in particular, the RBA’s near-term assumptions are downbeat compared to recent
demographic trends and also sit below Treasury’s longer-term projections as set out in the 2026 IGR.
As above, the August LFS emphasises that slack could emerge more quickly than the RBA’s forecasts
imply without an outright fall in employment.
Ahead of next week’s RBA meeting and inflation data, our latest state-level analysis provides

perspective on the persistence of underlying price pressures, particularly the importance of housing-
related components. For a broader assessment of the fiscal backdrop and the implications for the
states, our latest state fiscal update explores how the housing market correction and changes to NDIS
funding arrangements are pressuring state finances.
Before turning to this week’s events offshore, it is important to highlight that last Friday the Bank of

Japan raised its policy rate by 25bp to 1.25%. Policymakers maintained a constructive assessment of
the economy, pointing to resilient consumption, ongoing labour market tightness, and solid investment
spending. More importantly, the Bank expressed growing confidence that inflation is increasingly being
sustained by domestic wage and demand dynamics rather than imported cost shocks alone.
In our view, the emphasis placed on improving underlying inflation dynamics suggests policymakers

are becoming increasingly comfortable that inflation is able to be sustained by domestic demand and
wage growth. Normalisation is likely to remain gradual, but the Bank appears firmly on a path towards
further tightening, with rates likely to reach 1.5% by mid-2027.
This week, market participants have focused on discussions between the US and China on trade,
security and technology as President Xi visits Washington.
Preliminary discussions between US Treasury Secretary Bessent and Chinese Vice Premier He Lifeng

ahead of President Xi’s arrival in Washington were characterised by both sides as productive, and
Treasury Secretary Bessent subsequently announced that discussions in Washington would leave
trade negotiations for upcoming meetings between the two leaders in Shenzhen and Miami. To allow
room for this deferral, the current trade truce will be extended until January 2027. President Trump
subsequently outlined that discussions during President Xi’s Washington visit would instead focus on

security, technology and AI.
A more constructive tone was also seen between the US and Iran overnight, if press reports are
accurate. According to Bloomberg and Reuters, sources suggest the US and Iran are again exploring a
staged deal which would see Iran re-open the Strait of Hormuz and the US lift its blockade of Iranian
ports. Negotiations are occurring on the sidelines of the UN General Assembly, with Qatari officials

### Page 3

Related articles
mediating.
President Trump continues to show little interest in a deal before November’s mid-term elections, but
the global economy is under increasing pressure as the price of Brent oil hovers between USD100 and
USD110 while refining spreads add additional impetus to refined product prices. The mooted
consideration of a potential US diesel export ban this week makes clear that the US is not immune
from this pressure despite having significant domestic energy supply and refining capacity, relative to

their own demand.
The consequences of the current energy supply shock and other disruptions the US have experienced
over the past year remain front of mind for FOMC members. Those speaking this week such as NY and
Cleveland Fed Presidents Williams and Hammack highlighted the risk to inflation expectations from a
series of supply shocks when the economy is fully employed, and the likelihood of an additional hike(s)
being required to rein in current inflation momentum and risks. Williams noted that expectations of

another hike by year end are "reasonable" and that the FOMC have "a lot of work to do". Debt market’s
concern remains acute, having now almost priced in four hikes over the coming year and taken the US
10-year above 5.00%, currently 5.20%.

### Page 1

GROWTH MOMENTUM TO SUSTAIN, 
EXTERNAL RISKS LINGER

India registered  strong growth at the start of 
FY27. Sustaining this performance will depend 
increasingly on how effectively it navigates a 
more uncertain global environment. Besides 
trade fragmentation, geopolitical tensions 
leading to higher energy prices, and shifts in 
international capital flows present key external 
risks.

India joined its Southeast Asian peers in 
posting an upside surprise on growth in 
1QFY27 (Apr -Jun26), up a firm 7.8%  yoy. few 
points of note:  
A) strong 1Q growth print reinforced the view 
that India’s domestic demand cycle was more 
resilient than initially assumed, with 
consumption, public capex and manufacturing 
providing a solid base despite external shocks.  
B) prevailing tailwinds by way of indirect tax 
relief in 4Q25, real rate buffer, and easier 
monetary conditions (earlier cuts), were also 
supportive;  
C) nominal GDP growth returned above 10% 
after two years on higher deflators, though 
some segments like manufacturing faced 
negative deflators as input prices rose at a 
faster pace than output due to commodity price 
increases and import intensity;  
D) Revisions to backdated numbers, to account 
for output PPI series, pushed up FY26 growth to 
7.8% (from 7.7% earlier). Two prior years were 
also revised.

www.dbs.com/insightsdirect/macro groupresc@dbs.com 
• High frequency indicators 
might keep  2QFY growth 
close to 7%. 
• Pace to moderate in 2HFY. 
• Retail inflation is likely to 
continue ris e and broaden  in 
scope, necessitating a tighter 
policy bias.  WPI likely to r ise 
by double-digits. 
• Special swap windows helped 
to stem bearish INR 
positioning. 
• Organic capital flows need to 
catch up.  
• Liquidity management is in 
focus, with a moderate 
correlation to inflation. 
• Twin-deficit risks are partly 
offset by stronger macro 
buffers.

India: Growth, liquidity and policy

Radhika Rao, Senior Economist 
radhikarao@dbs.com

### Page 2

India: Growth, liquidity and policy response September 22, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

High-frequency indicators

Into July -August, high frequency 
indicators are holding up, including GST 
e-way bills, indirect tax  revenues, 
electricity demand and growth in digital 
payments, while toll collections and 
petroleum consumption slipped . For 
demand, a contained extent of increase 
in retail pump prices shielded 
households from the surge in global 
crude prices, but other market linked 
fuel variants have climbed in recent 
months. On the employment front, job 
reductions were witnessed in the 
software sector, even as fresh 
opportunities were reported in global 
capability centres , electr onics/ 
semiconductors, healthcare, auto/ 
ancillary etc.  Wage growth in services 
has improved , manufacturing has been 
steady, while farm sector moderated, 
according t o consumption surveys by 
CMIE.

Industry indicators are a mixed bag, with 
PMIs maintaining their foothold in the 
expansionary terrain, while production 
and eight core index growth come off 
highs. Auto production continues to 
expand, though output and sales might 
moderate in the next quarter on base 
effects due to indirect tax cuts in the 
same time year ago. Forward-looking 
firm surveys have improved, after 
initially coming under pressure.

Sectors associated with rural demand  
might moderate amid weaker kharif 
sowing activity and lower reservoir 
levels impacting the rabi crop activity. 
Cumulative southwest monsoon is ~15% 
below the long-term average by tail end 
of the season. This takes the country on 
course to face its driest monsoon since 
2009 (which was 18% below LPA). 
Uneven geographical spread is notable, 
with south and east India registering 
nearly 25% shortfall.  While k harif 
sowing is on track with 99% of acreage 
completed, the winter crop might come 
under pressure as weather 
developments suggest El Niño  could 
extend into 2027.

### Page 3

India: Growth, liquidity and policy response September 22, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

With nominal GDP growth to track 11-12% 
this fiscal yea r, corporate earning s 
growth is expected to fare better than 
recent few years. 1QFY27 performance 
had already set off the year on a strong 
note, barring selected sectors like O&G 
which faced sequential marginal 
pressures from operating costs. Add to 
this, the centre’s capex gr owth is in firm 
double-digits, also backed by growth in 
core imports (non-oil non-gold).

On the external sector , despite better 
performance by exports, oil and 
electronics purchases have weighed on 
the goods math. The trade deficit is set 
to stay wide amidst elevated global 
crude cost, higher industrial fuels, 
cooking gas etc,  even as a wider mix of 
suppliers have addressed energy 
shortages. Notably, the net electronics 
deficit has widened considerably, just 
as exports have improved. Likewise, core 
imports (ex oil ex gold) have stayed firm, 
which partly feeds into higher exports as 
well as broader domestic demand. 
Monthly service trade surplus has been 
rangebound around $17 -18bn in recent 
months dwarfed by $25 -$30bn goods 
gap.

Maintaining growth forecasts

High frequency indicators put 2QFY 
growth close to 7%. We expect full year 
FY27 growth to average 7.3% yoy  from 
a revised 7.8% in FY26 , assuming 
moderation in the momentum in the 
second half of the year on tighter policy 
conditions, lagged impact of high energy 
prices and base effects from indirect tax 
cuts last year fade.  Assuming some 
spillover impact of exogenous 
uncertainties and tight financial 
conditions at home, we expect FY28 
growth to average 6.8-7.0%.

INFLATION OFF ITS BACK – 
THREE CATALYSTS

After two years of benign inflation, 
India’s price trend is off its back this 
year, mainly on the back of three

### Page 4

India: Growth, liquidity and policy response September 22, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

catalysts: Food, energy and early signs 
of demand pass through.

Aug inflation rose to a 20 -month high of 
4.8% yoy from 4.5% month before . As 
the chart highlights, number of sub -
segments growing by faster than the 
inflation target at 4% have continued to 
climb, pointing to a broadening out in the 
momentum.

Food inflation is gradually becoming 
more broad -based, shifting beyond 
vegetables and pulses to sugar, milk, 
protein groups, edible oils, amongst 
others. Sub-par weather has contributed 
to this upmove, besides idiosyncratic 
factors like swing in production  trends, 
diversion for fuel usage etc. After two 
years of normal monsoon, cumulative 
southwest rainfall this year is currently 
15% below the long -term average, 
alongside slower build -up in reservoir 
levels, which could impact upcoming rabi 
crops as well.  El-Nino patterns continue 
to strengthen, adding to seasonal swings 
and risk of impacting farm output.

Beyond food, g lobal oil prices have 
averaged ~$91pb in CY26, staying above 
$100pb for much of Seo26 . Retail fuel 
prices were adjusted in quick succession 
back in May26 and have been left 
unchanged since then , while CNG, 
aviation fuel, LPG etc have  continued to 
climb.

Transport inflation jumped to 4.6% yoy in 
Aug vs 0.1% in Jan26, besides utilities’ 
component inching up. Business price 
expectation surveys (conducted by IIM 
Ahmedabad) have, nonetheless, 
captured businesses facing a notable 
increase in fuel costs, especially 
industrial fuels, base metals and 
derivative product groups. Imported 
price pressures are most notabl e in the 
producer price/ wholesale price indices. 
These gauges have been hovering at 9 -
10% yoy in recent months.

### Page 5

India: Growth, liquidity and policy response September 22, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

Lastly, core inflation  (ex-food and fuel)  
rose to 1.5-month high in August, ma inly 
due to a sequential rebound in precious 
metals – gold and silver jewellery. 
Excluding precious metals, the reading 
was more moderate at 2.9%, pointing to 
some extent of economic slack. 
Nonetheless, other pockets like telecom, 
restaurants, business services, transport 
fares etc have been inching up, 
reflecting early signs of broadening in 
price pressures (on sequential basis), 
warranting attention. These are set 
against the backdrop of strong bank 
credit growth, flush INR liquidity and 
resilience in headline growth.

Our correlation analysis between 
liquidity (as % of NDTL) and core inflation 
– see chart, points to a positive but 
modest corelation at 0.4 between the 
two variables, with an assumed year -
worth lag on inflation. The inflation -
fighting mandate will also pus h the 
central bank to reduce the surplus 
liquidity in the domestic system.

A sh arp in global commodity prices is 
expected to fe ed into the wholesale 
price gauge , pushing up reading s to 
double-digits next quarter.

A gradual broadening of price 
pressures is likely to keep headline 
inflation above 5% in second half of the 
fiscal year , sharply narrowing the real 
rate buffer, underscoring the need for a 
tighter policy bias. Add to this, recent 
developments, including a sustained rise 
in crude prices, tightening global 
financial conditions, firm domestic 
growth and signs of broadening in core 
pressures, strengthen the case for a  
shallow 50bp hike in second half of FY27, 
making October’s meeting a live one.

TACKLING LIQUIDITY DELUGE, 
HIGHER YIELDS

Beating street estimates by a wide 
margin, the RBI’s special swap windows 
raised a cumulative $ 143bn into Sep , 
with the highest contribution (~92%) by

### Page 6

India: Growth, liquidity and policy response September 22, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

FCNR(B) deposits at $1 33bn, while rest 
were via offshore borrowing facilities. 
These flows pushed up the foreign 
reserves stock to a fresh high past 
$780bn, providing considerable 
firepower to defend the currency. This is 
already noticeable in the recent shift in 
the intervention bias. USDINR, which was 
earlier stubbornly steady in the face of 
strong FCNR inflows but  eased sharply 
due to strong intervention dollar sales 
and broader dollar swings, before being 
led higher due to external triggers.

Liquidity is a key watch factor. Given the 
swap arrangement, these inflows have 
added to already abundant INR liquidity 
backdrop, which was at a four year high, 
depressing overnight rates. The banking 
system surplus jumped past INR 10trn 
surpassing 2022 and Covid highs, with 
the durable balance widening sharply 
higher to INR 14trn, suppressing 
overnight call rates. This compares to 
average INR 1trn surplus in late-June.

While organic drivers like tax -related 
outflows, and seasonal currency leakage, 
in addition to CAD, portfolio outflows, 
and maturity of the forwards book will 
act as counter -balancing factors, yet 
concerted steps were required to drain 
the potential surge in liquidity.

We discussed the various seasonal, 
near-term and permanent measures  
that the central bank might consider (see 
FCNR bonanza necessitates deft 
management) to absorb this surfeit. 
Policymakers are being mindful of both 
the liquidity and signalling effects of 
the sterilisation tools at their disposal. 
The RBI utilised tranches of market 
operations like VRRR across tenors, 
after which three tranches of open 
market operations (bonds maturing FY29 
and FY32) worth a cumulative INR 1trn 
were conducted. These announcements 
fuelled a rise in yields, which were 
already inching up, tracking global 
counterparts. Sell/buy FX swaps for  
shorter rollovers were also being 
reportedly tapped.

When the dust settles, focus will also 
be on the bunched -up maturities that 
will fall due in 3Y and 5Y tenor of the 
deposits. A portion of the existing 
reserve stock could be earmarked 
against these liabilities, helping to 
mitigate concerns that deposit 
maturities or debt repayments could 
trigger a sharp increase in dollar demand 
and exert pressure on the FX market 
down the line. In the near-term, priorities 
will be to manage liquidity, gradually 
lower the sizeable forwards book, and 
support the domestic currency. Long -
end yields continue to be influenced by 
the hardening global yields.

BALANCE OF PAYMENTS MATH 
TO BE COMFORTABLE

The FY27 current account math is 
expected to display two key counter 
trends, Firstly, the wider goods trade 
imbalance will raise the FY27 current 
account deficit. Secondly, the 
counterbalancing effect of a sharp 
improvement in the capital flows math 
will keep the annual balance of 
payments math in surplus.

### Page 7

India: Growth, liquidity and policy response September 22, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            7

India recorded a current account deficit 
(CAD) of $10.5bn during April-July 2026, 
compared with $6.1bn in the 
corresponding period last year. The 
deterioration was driven almost entirely 
by a wider merchandise trade deficit, 
which rose to $117.8bn from $97.1bn a 
year ago. Part of this offset by higher  
income, transfers and capital account 
flow. Capital flows found support from 
net FDI, portfolio investments and 
banking capital, especially NRI deposits 
(jumped sharply to $36bn vs $4.6bn 
same period last year) , pushing up Apr -
Jul26 BOP to $12.7bn surplus, more than 
double of $4.8bn same time last year.

Oil prices are the main swing factor for 
the CAD this year , with the current run -
rate tracking our forecast at -1.1% of GDP 
for FY27. This suggests that the 
widening in the CAD is commodity -price 
related rather than driven by higher 
demand.

On the other hand, capital flows are 
being buttressed by the special swap 
windows introduced by the central bank, 
which raised a cumulative $ 143bn 
between June and August, through non -
resident deposits and external 
borrowings. This has helped to fil l the 
gap from subdued portfolio flows and 
net investment trends. We expect FY27 
current account deficit to track -1.1% of 
GDP in FY27, with the balance of 
payments to register upwards of $80bn 
surplus (vs -$23.6bn deficit last year).  
Into FY28, the dependency of capital 
flows on o rganic drivers like portfolio 
flows and FDI is set to increase, 
underscoring the need for higher 
vigilance.

FISCAL RUN-RATE

The FY27 fiscal run -rate points to a 
stronger revenue momentum, which 
has helped to keep the FYTD deficit in 
check, even as expenditure was 
frontloaded owing to subsidy payments 
and capex disbursements. April-July 
2026 deficit stands at a third of the full-
year balance.

### Page 8

India: Growth, liquidity and policy response September 22, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            8

Both tax and non -tax receipts fared 
better than the comparable period year 
ago, helped by stronger corporate tax 
collections, STT transaction revenues, 
and RBI dividend, amongst others.

Setting 2QFY for a strong start, net 
direct tax collections r ose 13% yoy by 
mid-Sep26, driven by net corporate tax 
collections up 19.5%. Net non -corporate 
tax collections were up 6%, besides STT 
collections up a sharp 53% yoy. Add to 
this, divestments have also fared better 
than expected this year, with ~78% 
raised by August, accompanied by 
dividends & profits (at 83% of budget by 
Jul26).

Under expenditure, about 33% of the full 
year allocation was allocated by July 
2026. The subsidy outgo rose sharply, up 
35% yoy, led by higher food at 23% and 
fertiliser at 46% (already accounting for 
nearly two-third of the full year budget)  
led by surging global DAP and urea 
prices. With global prices remaining high, 
the actual outgo might be higher than 
budgeted INR 1.7trn , likely towards INR 
2.5trn. Capital spending also remained 
apace, up 30% yoy in first five months of 
the year.

Overall, strong growth in tax and non-tax 
receipts allowed the centre to sustain 
higher spending while containing the 
extent of fiscal slippage until July 2026 
(FYTD). A strong nominal GDP rate 
(helped by higher inflation) will also 
provide a favourable denominator effect.

IN SUM

The economy exhibited resilience in the 
face of a tough external environment in 
the first quarter, with part of that 
stability likely to extend to 2QFY27 as 
well. Tighter monetary conditions, El 
Nino impact and base effects from 
passage of post -indirect tax jump will 
moderate headline growth in 2HFY. 
Nonetheless for the year, we expected 
growth to stay north of 7%, factoring in a 
strong start.

                                                                                                                                            9

S&P 500 SECTORS CALL: More Green On The Screen

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
S&P 500 SECTORS CALL: More
Green On The Screen
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 11 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Bond yields edged down last week. As a result, the S&P 500 edged up,
led by interest-rate-sensitive sectors. The AI-related sectors Industrials
(-0.4%) and Information Technology (-0.3%) edged down and in fact were
the only down sectors last week. The big interest-rate-sensitive gainers
were Consumer Staples (4.0%), Utilities (3.9%), Consumer Discretionary
(3.1%), and Financials (2.3%).
The year's laggards were last week's leaders (chart).
Let's have a look at the Energy, Financials, and Industrials sectors:

(1) Energy. S&P 500 Energy (3.6%) powered ahead on renewed risks to
ships passing through the Strait of Hormuz (chart). Over the weekend, the
Houthis attacked King Khalid International Airport in Riyadh and declared
Saudi airspace a zone of military operations, warning airlines, travelers,
and airport workers to steer clear of airports across the kingdom.
Sector momentum turned decisively positive mid-week, bolstered by a
late-week surge as energy equities reacted to escalating Middle East

conflicts and subsequent spikes in crude oil benchmarks (chart). Brent
crude is quiet tonight at $103.65 per barrel. President Donald Trump
helped calm the oil market by announcing that an energy ceasefire
between Russia and Ukraine took effect immediately, saying on social
media that both parties had agreed to the terms.

### Page 3

The rebound in the Energy sector's forward earnings this year has been
attributable to the jump in the sector's forward profit margin, fueled by the
jump in oil prices (chart).
The sector's forward earnings is up 61.7% y/y, and its stock price index is
up 53.1% (chart). There should be more upside for the rest of the year,
especially if the war in the Middle East heats up after the mid-term
elections, as Trump has threatened.

### Page 4

Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing has been the sector's big winner so far
this year (chart). A global shortage of refinery capacity might fuel more
gains.
(2) Financials. The Q3-2026 earnings reporting season starts this week
with the big banks. The sector's stock price index is nearly down at its
200-day moving average (chart). Solid earnings reports from the banks
are likely, as we explained last week. If so, then the sector's uptrend
should hold.

### Page 5

Rising interest rates are probably boosting the net interest margins of the
large banks. However, the rapid ascent in bond yields in recent weeks has
unnerved investors, as evidenced by the sharp drop in the sector's
forward P/E since mid-August (chart). Bond yields may be stabilizing as
investors are drawn to higher returns.
The sector's forward earnings is up 17.3% y/y, while its stock price index
is up only 4.9% y/y (chart). A solid Q3 earnings season should lift the

sector.

### Page 6

So far this year, insurance industries have performed best in the sector
(chart).
(3) Industrials. Industrials balanced competing forces this week—
benefiting from safe-haven rotation and robust structural demand, while
facing occasional headwinds from surging energy input costs and
persistent high-interest-rate pressures. This S&P 500 sector is also testing
support at its 200-day moving average (chart).

### Page 7

This is another sector where forward earnings is rising faster than the
sector's stock price index (chart). The price action sometimes leads the
growth rate in forward earnings. This time may be different as the AI
buildout continues to boost the sector's earnings.
Construction Machinery & Heavy Trucks is the sector's best-performing
industry so far this year (chart). Second best has been Rail
Transportation. Notwithstanding the wars in the Middle East and between

Russia and Ukraine, Aerospace & Defense has been the weakest
industry. Military budgets for large munitions and battlefield weapons may
have to make room for more spending on AI-guided drones and
"Terminators."

《歐洲央行9月會議如預期升息一碼，12月再次升
息可能性提高》
歐洲央行為了抑制通膨 ，2026年以來已升息二碼 ，
將存款機制利率上調至 2.5%。在歐央最新基準情
境預測中，2026、2027年歐元區GDP成長率預測
上修至0.9%、1.4%，然而，中東衝突持續、荷姆
茲海峽航運受阻及能源價格持續波動下 ，歐元區
經濟前景仍高度不確定。
通膨預估將在更長時間高於政策目標 ，基準情境
預估通膨率將在 4Q26升至3.6%高峰後，2Q27降
至2.5%。能源價格上升的影響將逐步傳導至非能
源價格 ，核心通膨預估 1Q27升至2.8%峰值後才
緩步回落，2027、2028年核心通膨率預測分別上

修0.1個百分點至 2.6%、2.3%，高於目標水準 。
不過，透過薪資傳導的二輪效應預期仍有限。
後續歐央政策將取決於能源價格衝擊的強度與持
續時間 ，以及是否進一步傳導至核心通膨 、薪資
與通膨預期。本行預期9月升息後，10月會議歐洲
央行將傾向觀望 ，然而，布蘭特原油期貨價格維
持在90美元/桶以上高檔 ，歐洲TTF天然氣期貨價
格漲幅更甚原油 ，在經濟維持韌性下 ，能源價格
維持高檔時間拉長 、核心通膨顯現能源價格的間
接傳導若增強 ，本行預估12月會議歐洲央行高機
率將再升息一碼，將存款機制利率升至2.75%。
資料來源：ECB，2026/9/10，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/9。
歐洲央行上修經濟成長與通膨預測

重點摘要
4
• 公債殖利率跳升對歐股評價
帶來壓力。
《公債殖利率跳升，歐股承壓》
九月份以來全球公債殖利率大幅攀升，對歐股造成
壓力。德國十年期公債殖利率9月份至9/28止跳升
32個基點至3.64%，創2009年6月以來新高。若從
2月底中東地緣衝突爆發以來起算 ，德國十年債利
率累積彈升了近100個基點。本波歐洲公債殖利率
走高來自多重因素疊加，能源價格上漲推升通膨、
市場重估歐央貨幣政策路徑，同時，財政支出令歐
洲公債供給增加與歐央持續縮表使私人部門需要吸
收更多公債供給，推升期限溢價，財政疑慮使部分
歐洲國家利差承受額外壓力，加上全球利率上升的
外溢影響，共同推升殖利率走高。
公債殖利率彈升令歐洲股市評價受到壓抑 ，9月份

以來至9/28止，STOXX歐洲600指數下跌近 2%。
類股中除了能源類股隨油價走高而漲逾 5%，銀行
股收紅外，其餘類股普跌，汽車及零件、基本資源
與零售股下跌7%以上。

《能源價格與利率走勢、預算協商、財報牽動10月
歐股盤勢》
展望10月份，美伊衝突的進展以及能源價格和債券
殖利率的走勢、企業財報、 預算協商將是盤面焦點。
能源價格衝擊與通膨傳導將是牽動後續利率的主要
因素，中東僵局持續令能源價格維持高檔更久，除
非中東局勢迅速平息，否則12月歐央可能再次升息，
公債殖利率短期仍將維持在高檔。
預算協商亦將對市場帶來干擾，9/28十年期法德利
差已跳升至 112.7個基點，創2012年6月以來新高 ，
主要反映市場對法國財政狀況的擔憂，今年第二季
法國政府債務佔GDP比重已逼近120%。

法國總理勒克努將於 10/1提出2027年預算案 ，法
國政府目標力拚節省540億歐元開支、將2027年財
政赤字佔GDP的比重降至5%。在政治局勢高度分
裂、2027年將舉行法國大選之際，此計劃將面臨強
大的政治阻力。無論是2027年預算討論、法國大選
民調的變化，相關不確定性預估將牽動短期內十年
期法德利差維持在約100個基點的高檔，無明顯回
落空間。
資料來源：巴克萊資本，2026/8/28，統計過去三次(2012、2017、2022年)法國選舉
周期，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9。

《新歐：獲利預估改善 ，天然氣價格大漲帶來壓
力》
MSCI新興歐洲指數9月以來回檔1.2%，波蘭與希
臘股市上揚 ，然而 ，土耳其股匯市同步承壓 ，
MSCI土耳其股市美元計價下跌 4.2%，拖累新歐
指數收黑(美元計價，統計至9/28止)。
土耳其資本市場委員會 2026年8月收緊了對對沖
基金及資產管理公司的監管政策 ，新法限制基金
對單一成分股及流動性差股票的持倉上限 ，並強
制要求在年底前完成清算超額倉位。9月中旬爆發
贖回危機 ，多家土耳其資產管理公司未能滿足投
資人贖回請求 ，土耳其資本市場委員會下令清算
七家資產管理公司管理的 131檔基金 ，合計資產

管理規模約1兆里拉，土耳其央行同步採取措施提
供市場流動性 ，儘管不至於對廣泛金融體系構成
系統性風險 ，但短線土耳其股市 ，尤其是小型股
的波動預估仍將較為劇烈。
近一個月新歐企業獲利預估改善 ，以波蘭上修幅
度較大 ，希臘居次 ，土耳其相對較小 ，市場預估
新歐企業 2026、2027年獲利將分別成長 19%及
17%(高盛證券，9/4)。
然而，TTF天然氣價格已突破 3月份高點。從新興
股市與天然氣價格的敏感度來看 ，新歐股市相對
較易受到天然氣價格大漲的負面影響 ，且目前新
歐股市估值已回升至高於5年平均水準，對新歐股
市維持中立看法。

主題報告概述 : 本篇報告將分析 2026年美元現況 ，最終形成
2027年美元看法。以下為本篇報告內容概要:
(1) 匯市現況：美國伊朗戰爭導致通膨疑慮再起，美元走強。
(2) 政治議題：2026年共和黨失去絕對執政對美元的影響。
(3) 期中選後風險因子：財政、貿易戰、美伊戰爭。
(4) 美元基本面：景氣與通膨循環將維持在金髮女孩經濟環境。
(5) 美元技術面：技術面跌破年線，籌碼面偏多但8月轉弱。
(6) 非美匯市: 歐洲政治面利空及日本阻貶為美元提供底部支撐
匯市結論: 美元已從2026年初的降息交易轉向受到美伊戰爭 、

油價上漲及通膨預期升溫支撐 ，但此波強勢更多來自地緣政治
與高利率環境 ，而非美國基本面出現結構性轉強 。期中選舉後 ，
共和黨失去完全執政優勢 ，國會分裂將限制大規模財政擴張 ，
有助抑制通膨與赤字惡化 ，但也降低進一步刺激經濟成長的動
能。展望2027年，美國仍面臨債務上限 、財政協商、貿易政策、
美伊衝突及烏俄戰爭等風險 ，其中地緣政治與油價走勢仍是左
右美元的重要變數 。2027年美元並非進入新一輪強勢多頭週期 ，
而是在高利率支撐下維持區間整理 。2027年美國經濟基準情境
穩健，景氣溫和成長 ，通膨從高檔放緩 。若無重大地緣政治升
級事件，美元指數預期維持97–105區間震盪，並呈現「上有壓
力、下有支撐 」的盤整格局 。當地緣政治升溫時美元偏強 ，當
通膨回落與利率下降時美元偏弱。
資料來源： 中國信託商業銀行整理。2026/9

(1)匯市現況 : 美伊戰爭導致通膨疑慮再起，
美元走強
匯市現況: 2026年美元走勢主要受到美國經濟基本面、Fed貨幣
政策預期、川普政策以及地緣政治風險交互影響。
2026年初，川普重返貿易壁壘政策 、美國就業動能放緩且通膨
趨穩，市場普遍預期降息循環延續 ，使美元維持偏弱整理格局 ，
美元指數於1月27日一度跌至全年低點95.55。期間宣布華許出
任Fed主席的消息曾短暫帶動美元反彈 ，但整體仍未擺脫區間
震盪格局 。然而，2月28日伊朗事件爆發 ，3月9日西德州原油
一度接近每桶 120美元，能源價格上升帶動全球通膨預期升溫 ，
美國降息預期下降。進入3-4月，美國經濟數據進一步展現韌性，
製造業 、消費及投資表現改善 ，Fed季度預測亦同步上修 GDP

成長與通膨展望 。受此影響 ，利率期貨隱含年底前降息預期由
原先降息2碼逐步修正至不降息 ，推動美元於2Q26展開反彈。
同時 ，受油價飆升影響 ，9月Fed季度預測將今年 PCE上修至
3.7%，官員點陣圖亦上修利率路徑 ，市場開始討論通膨壓力可
能迫使Fed重新升息 。匯市焦點亦逐漸轉向新任聯準會主席華
許提出的新貨幣政策框架 。在Fed政策預期持續調整下 ，美元
指數於6月24日升至全年高點101.8，隨後進入高檔盤整格局。

(2)政治議題：2026年共和黨失去絕對執政對
美元的影響(2/3)
數席次機率下行趨勢可見 ，當川普政策面無法政治上快速解決
問題並且影響民生時，取得參眾議院的機率加速下行。
期中選舉結果對美元影響 ：觀察1970年以來美國期中選舉結束
後一年美元之平均表現。
民主黨主導兩院 ：當民主黨期中選舉後在參眾議院均為多數 ，
美元指數平均下跌 -4.6%，6次有4次為下跌 ，且前一年美元均
下跌。1971、1987及2007年為下跌幅度最大的3年。
1971年尼克森(共和黨)總統宣布停止黃金兌換 ，10%進口附加
關稅，迫使盟友重啟貿易與貨幣談判(最終達成史密松寧協定)，
美元下跌-7.8%。

1987年雷根(共和黨)延續1985年廣場協議後美元貶值趨勢延續 。
雙赤字壓力 、通膨升溫 、利率上升以及外資拋售壓力導致發生
1987年10月19日黑色星期一股市崩盤 ，美元指數當年下跌 -
17.6%(1986年下跌-16.1%)。
2007年布希(共和黨)總統時期面臨雙赤字 、非美利差縮小 ，以
及次貸風暴風險升溫。美元下跌-8.3%(2006年下跌-8.2%)。

Thematic Report
重點提示
(2)政治議題：2026年共和黨失去絕對執政對
美元的影響(3/3)
共和黨、民主黨各主導一議院 ：當美國國會進入兩黨各主導一
議院時期，美元指數期中選舉過後一年平均上漲 3.6%，4次有3
次為上漲，且前一年4次美元均為上漲。美國期中選舉若造成國
會由不同政黨掌控 ，通常會限制總統隨意推動大規模擴張性財
政政策與赤字支出 。這類財政節制有助於降低通膨失控風險 ，
進而支撐美元走強。4次國會分裂時段其中較大美元升值趨勢為
1983年雷根(共和黨)時期。 1980年以來聯準會主席保羅伏克爾
採取強力升息手段打擊通膨 ，美國利率保持在相對高檔一段時
間且通膨已逐步受到控制 (從1980年15%降至4%以下)。美元

1983年升值14.9%(1982年升值12.1%)。1983年後，美國經濟
順利擺脫先前的嚴重衰退 ，展開強勁增長 ，市場對美國資產信
心大增，形成所謂強勢美元格局 。美元指數在80年代高點轉折
時期美國實質利率(基準利率-消費者物價指數)已從高檔下降。
小結：2026年期中選舉結果可能形成國會分裂 ，國會分裂長期
有助抑制赤字與通膨 ，因此長期對美元有利 ，但短期可能因預
算協商及債務上限僵局增加政治風險 ，對美元造成波動。2027
年將再度面臨美國債務上限 ，以及美伊地緣政治 、貿易戰的變
數均將牽動實質利率的變化 ，因此接下來將進一步分析選後影
響美元的關鍵因子。
✓

對美元有利
✓

隨美元趨勢走弱
✓

Thematic Report
重點提示
(3)期中選後風險因子：財政、貿易戰、美伊戰
爭(1/3)
期中選舉過後影響美元的關鍵因子聚焦 財政面 (政府關門事件)、
貿易戰(法院裁定關稅無效後宣布以其他方式課徵關稅)、地緣政
治面(伊朗戰爭觸發油價上揚)。
財政面：8月國會通過持續撥款決議至 12月11日，代表政府關
門的風險將落在11月3日後，但新國會於2027年1月3日任職，
因此很可能選後再延後撥款決議 。除此之外 ，根據兩黨政策中
心預測，2027年9月後可能再度面臨美國債務上限議題 ，屆時
面臨2028年預算審核，參眾議院的分治恐延長達成共識的時間
以及提升美國主權債信評或展望調降風險 。從全國納稅人聯盟

(NTU)情境分析預測，美國聯邦政府收支很可能在2027年2月至
7月觸及債務上限。一旦債務達到法定限額，財政部長將宣布暫
停發行債務期 。在暫停發行債務期間內 ，財政部不得增加受法
定限額約束的債務 ，但到期債務仍可展期 。計算美國財政部透
過動用收入 、現有現金儲備和採取非常規措施 ，真正無法履行
聯邦義務約在2028年2月。然而債務上限和債務違約日(X-Date)
何時達到存在很大的不確定性 。受債務上限約束的債務將於
2027年3月接近法定上限 500億美元以內，之後隨著4月稅收的
到來而暫時下降 ，但若政府財政赤字增加超過原先預測 ，X-
Date將會提前到來。

Thematic Report
重點提示
(3)期中選後風險因子：財政、貿易戰、美伊戰
爭(2/3)
美國信評方面 ，惠譽8月將美國信評維持在 AA+評級且展望穩
定，但警告信評下調風險因子如下 ：政府債務佔GDP水準或償
債能力惡化 ，例如未能解決公共支出和收入的平衡 ，或遭受宏
觀金融衝擊 。經濟政策制定可信度下降導致削弱美元儲備貨幣
地位，從而降低政府融資彈性 。惠譽預期 ，美國債務占GDP比
重到2028年可能升至123%，較AA評級國家46.3%的債務比率
中值高出一倍以上 ，因此，美國信評或展望下修的風險有可能
同時伴隨財政惡化或經濟成長放緩 /衰退造成償債能力惡化 。
2027年美國財政議題將繼續面臨逆風 ，共和黨失去完全執政的

環境對財政有利但政治僵局則凸顯債務上限及違約的風險 ，因
此增加美元短期波動風險。
貿易戰：2026年川普貿易戰從對等關稅全面性課徵轉向法律攻
防，預期2027年貿易政策以各國與美國雙邊貿易協定談判為主
軸，以及針對進口品項實施不同的關稅 (川普8月宣布2027年起
對一系列加拿大汽車產品加徵最高達 50%的關稅)。川普貿易政
策不確定性因素延續 ，但本行認為時序進入 2027年貿易戰全面
性惡化的可能性已降低 ，且企業及投資人已對貿易政策不確定
性有一定程度的認知 ，因此傳導至資產價格的影響性亦將有所
降低。
地緣政治 ：地緣政治風險的擴散效應高於上述財政及關稅 。主
因荷姆茲海峽能源輸出量佔全球 20%，油價上漲對通膨的影響
為2027年美元走勢的主要變數 。2月底事件爆發以來原油遠期
曲線現貨溢價 ，代表短期內市場需求強烈或發生供給中斷 。目
前美伊戰爭從熱戰轉向封鎖荷姆茲海峽 。UAE及部分中東國家
已開始運用地面輸油管線避開該管線 ，但伊朗無人機打擊範圍
遠至含蓋紅海 ，倘若事件面惡化 ，紅海運輸除了葉門胡塞武裝

組織干擾，亦有伊朗無人機攻擊風險。目前美伊談判仍未達成
✓

重點提示
(3)期中選後風險因子：財政、貿易戰、美伊戰
爭(3/3)
協議，伊朗拒絕交出高濃縮鈾 ，因此仍無法排除美國運用地面
部隊進行搜索 ，也無法排除伊朗透過遠端無人機攻擊美國在其
他中東國家的軍事或煉油設施 ，美國運用地面部隊恐將地緣政
治風險升級，屆時油價、利率以及通膨預期可能同步向上。
風險變數對2027年美元的影響 ：美國政府債務問題並非全新風
險因子。自1960年以來，美國國會已經累計調整法定債務上限
超過78次。然而，2027年若期中選後國會分裂 ，疊加年底預算
勢必面臨再延後撥款決議 ，屆時政府關門或違約的不確定性因
素將影響經濟成長並且降低聯準會緊縮的可能 。而美國債務上

限再度上調則可能引起信評展望調降的風險 。以美元的角度而
言，有紀律的財政計畫的確可將加強投資人對美元資產的信心 ，
對美元有利 。但短線若因此造成政府關門以及無法在預算達成
協議則對美元短線不利 ，因此定調2027年財政風險因子在分裂
國會環境，對美元而言屬於短空長多。
除了債務風險外 ，貿易戰及美伊戰爭事件的發展最終將反應在
油價及通膨的走勢。從今年油價一度飆升至每桶120美元可見，
短期油價的上漲並未造成短線通膨危機。但不可否認，高油價

若債務上限 、地緣政治 、貿易壁壘等議題未惡化 ，預期美國經
濟偏向溫和成長 ，通膨可望放緩 。觀察中國信託美國景氣動向
指數可見，3個月以來，股市動能略顯放緩，但多數領先指標仍
持續改善 。FactSet 預估2027年標普500企業獲利成長 15%，
且營收成長高達9%，企業獲利面維持高度擴張。樂觀的企業前
景繼續帶動資本支出 ，彭博社統計2027年標普500企業資本支
出年增率仍高達 20%，正向的企業獲利持續傳導到實際經濟 。
2024年以來，實際經濟數據持續改善 ，且今年已回到 0軸以上
擴張區間 。伴隨調查及價格指標持續向上 ，整體景氣動能趨勢

正向。 展望2027年，溫和成長伴隨通膨放緩美元處於盤整偏弱
格局，基本情境內貨幣政策則傾向維持基準利率穩定。

2026/9
義大利民粹政黨執政時期10
年公債殖利率與歐元區利差
30%，歐洲政治生態將進入高度不穩定與兩極化的狀態 。在歐
洲多黨內閣制或雙首長制國家中 ，30%是傳統主流政黨能否順
利執政的關鍵臨界點 。進入2027年，歐盟將有 7國舉行國會選

舉，1月30日德國總統選舉以及4月18日法國總統大選。民粹主
義右翼政黨正在崛起 ，並可能在多個國家大幅增加席次 。每次
選舉結果都將影響各國政治局勢 ，進而反映整個歐洲的政治環
境。然而，右翼民粹主義的崛起對金融市場也並非絕對利空 ，
義大利右翼政黨取得政權後堅持財政紀律 ，向金融市場展現負
責任的治理 ，同時關注移民和文化衝突等社會議題 ，以維繫基
層支持者 。義大利和歐元區 10年期公債利差從義大利右翼政黨
勝選時2.45%至今已收斂至0.83%。此外，許多右翼民粹主義政
黨也緩和了其疑歐立場 ，不再主張退出歐元區或歐盟 。2027年
法國總統大選為首要政治議題。法國極右翼領袖勒龐(Le Pen)與
她所屬的政黨國民聯盟 (RN)近年來調整了政綱 ，已放棄脫歐主
張，並且在財政上淡化激進政策 ，強調財政紀律將遵守歐盟規
範，將財政赤字控制在GDP 3%以下。整體而言，歐洲政治光譜
進入兩極化 ，法國選後議會仍需籌組多數聯盟 ，財政惡化風險
可控，但若極左派梅蘭雄意外勝選，大幅增加政府支出、對富

日本央行貨幣政策正常化路徑延續 。貝森特支持日本以美國公
債作為抵押 ， 讓 日 本 在 不 出 售 美 國 公 債 的 情 況 下 買 入 日 圓
(FIMA-外國與國際貨幣當局附買回機制 )。目前日本持有 1.1兆
美元公債 ，同時加上美國政府表態支持干預弱勢日圓 ，美日聯
手讓日圓短線貶勢暫時告一段落 。美元兌日圓9月在160再度形

成一波回檔，顯示美日政府力守160整點關卡的決心。目前日本
利率(無擔保隔夜拆款利率)1.25%水準再同時加入日圓隔夜指數交
換利率隱含升息3碼，2027年利率水準與歐美差距仍有一段距離，
實質利率仍為負值，且在成熟市場中偏低。除此之外，高市政府
擴張性財政政策及新增支出計畫恐持續不利日圓 。高市於7月30
日宣布自2027年4月起，將現行8%食品消費稅率降至1%，實施
期限為2年。兩年加總稅收將減少10兆日圓，以2025年總稅收而
言，每年約擴大赤字 -6%。除此之外 ，國防預算計畫每年以
2%GDP編列，預估2027年將達14兆日圓。同時，國家級長期戰
略投資計畫於2040年前投入370兆日圓支持17大戰略領域，整體
財政方針不利日圓中長期表現。

Thematic Report
重點提示
(6)非美匯市 : 歐洲政治不確定性及日本阻貶為
美元提供底部支撐(5/5)
✓
日圓長期轉為多頭
關鍵因子則將落在
日本政府是否開始
運用政策引導海外
投資資金回流
可視為第一波技術面日圓多方關卡。就基本面而言，政府干預匯
市無法改變貨幣政策與財政政策的路徑。若日本通膨無法帶動日
本央行加速升息，日圓利率水準則無法形成資金流入的誘因。以
日圓長期轉為多頭關鍵因子則將落在日本政府是否開始運用政策
引導海外投資資金回流(日本財務省統計到2025年底，日本在海
外的淨資產達近562兆日圓)，將是長期影響日圓走勢的最有效政
策。
匯市結論: 美元已從2026年初的降息交易轉向受到美伊戰爭、油

價上漲及通膨預期升溫支撐 ，但此波強勢更多來自地緣政治與
高利率環境 ，而非美國基本面出現結構性轉強 。期中選舉後 ，
共和黨失去完全執政優勢 ，國會分裂將限制大規模財政擴張 ，
有助抑制通膨與赤字惡化 ，但也降低進一步刺激經濟成長的動
能。展望2027年，美國仍面臨債務上限 、財政協商、貿易政策
及美伊衝突等風險 ，其中地緣政治與油價走勢仍是左右美元的
重要變數 。外部變數則關注烏俄戰爭是否停戰 、美日聯和干預
日圓與日本2028財年赤字規模 ，以及法國總統大選對歐洲政治
光譜的影響。
整體而言，2027年美元並非進入新一輪強勢多頭週期 ，而是在
高利率支撐下維持區間整理 。2027年美國經濟基準情境穩健 ，
景氣溫和成長 ，通膨從高檔放緩 。若無重大地緣政治升級事件 ，
美元指數預期維持 97-105區間震盪，並呈現「上有壓力、下有
支撐」的盤整格局 。當地緣政治升溫時美元偏強 ，當通膨回落
與利率下降時美元偏弱。

3Q26 缺料及 18KW 新品出貨調整，營收略低於預期 
3Q26 營收估達 2,093 億元，季增 14%，下修 6%，主因 PMIC/MOSFET 缺
料及 18KW PSU 驗證不順影響，估 11M26 可完全恢復供貨。液冷散熱營收
由 220 億上修至 250 億元，季持平，主因客戶需求強勁及其產品品質穩定。
毛利率估 36.3%，季增 0.6 ppts，下修 0.3 ppts，主因營收規模低於預期。
EPS 估 10.8 元，季增 11%，下修 4%。本中心認為 2Q-3Q26 為營運調整期，
持續排除包含缺料、料源漲價及供應商料況問題等負面因素。 
4Q26 漲價+新 GPU 平台電源加速出貨/HVDC 出貨使獲利成長 
估 4Q26 營收達 2,511 億元，季增 20%，下修 4%，主因 PMIC/MOSFET 缺
料狀況較預期嚴峻，然 4Q26 營收季增強主因 1) 新平台 AI 伺服器電源出貨
加速；2) 少量 HVDC 於 4Q26 出貨貢獻；3) 透過漲價反映成本(估漲幅達 5-
10%)。估 4Q26 毛利率達 38.7%，季增 2.4 ppts，下修 0.2 ppts。毛利率估
將創新高，主因上述三大成長動能均同步推升 GPM 成長，尤以 18KW PSU
及 HVDC Power Rack 兩新產品估毛利率均達 50%。估 EPS 達 14.7 元，季
增 36%，下修 1%。 
營運調整將過，漲價/高 W 數 PSU 及 HVDC 出貨加速挹注成長 
本中心預估台達電 2026/27 年營收年增 45%/61%至 8,030/12,893 億元，下
修 3/3%。下調 AI 營收 7%/5%至 3,407/7,656 億元，年增 197%/125%，反
映缺料問題。估台達電 2026/2027 年 EPS 43.1/80.8 元，年增 86%/88%，
下修 1%/上修 2%；另上修 2028 年 EPS 3% 至 121.6 元，看好 HVDC 將於
2H27 始大量出貨。估公司 2026-28 年 EPS 以 CAGR 68%成長，以 2027
年 EPS 80.8 元，30 倍本益比(自前次 28 倍調升，反映料況/成本/新品出貨
調整谷底將過，4Q26 起將受惠漲價/新平台 PSU 及 HVDC 出貨挹注成長)，
得目標價 2,430 元，買進評等。 
元大觀點 
⧫  
3Q26 缺料持續影響且 18KW 新品驗證不順，估 3Q26 EPS 10.8 元，
季增 11%，下修 4%。整體營運調整期估已近尾聲。 
⧫  
4Q26 漲價、新 GPU 平台電源及 HVDC 少量生產，估 EPS 季增 36%
至 14.7 元，下修 1%。營運調整已近尾聲，後續將逐季加速成長。 
⧫  
台達電將出貨 HVDC 系統級產品且其完整電源技術為 AIDC 電力瓶頸
關鍵。以 2027 年 EPS 80.8 元及 30 倍本益比，得目標價 2,430 元。

營運分析  
3Q26 缺料及伺服器電容供應商調整影響產能提升，液冷出貨估優於預期 
3Q26 營收估達 2,093 億元，季增 14%/年增 39%，下修 6%，3Q26 受 PMIC/MOSFET 缺料使出貨
無法滿足客戶需求及新平台 18KW PSU 驗證不順影響，主因日系電容供應商電流外溢問題，台達
電於 9M26 增加其他供應商供貨以補齊供貨，估將影響 1-2 個月供貨，11M26 方可完全恢復供貨。
液冷散熱 7-8M26 出貨達 162 億元，優於預期主因客戶需求強勁及其產品品質穩定，估 3Q26 液冷
散熱出貨自 220 億元上修至 250 億元，季持平。毛利率估 36.3%，季增 0.6/年增 1.4 個 ppts，下
修 0.3 個 ppts，主因營收規模略低於本中心預期。營益率估 18.7%，季增 2.0 個 ppts/年增 2.2 個
ppts，下修 0.3 個 ppts。EPS 估 10.8 元，季增 11%/年增 51%，下修 4%。本中心認為台達電陸續
排除包含缺料、料源漲價及供應商料況等負面因素，2Q-3Q26 為公司短期營運谷底。

4Q26 漲價/GPU 新平台出貨加速/HVDC 少量生產，估 EPS 季增 36%至 14.7 元 
本中心預估 4Q26 營收達 2,511 億元，季增 20%/年增 55%，微幅下修 4%，主因 PMIC/MOSFET
缺料狀況較預期嚴峻，然整體 4Q26 營收季增仍強勁且估續創新高，主因 1) 新平台 AI 伺服器電源
如 12KW/18KW 以上 AC/DC、On-Board DC/DC Converter 均出貨加速；2) 少量+-400V HVDC 於
4Q26 出貨貢獻；3) 公司透過漲價陸續反映成本於客戶(估漲價幅度達 5-10%)。估 4Q26 毛利率達
38.7%，季增 2.4/年增 4.1 個 ppts，下修 0.2 個 ppts。毛利率估將創新高，主因上述三大營收成長
動能均同步推升 GPM 成長，尤以 18KW PSU 及 HVDC Power Rack 兩新產品估毛利率均達 50%，
有助推升整體獲利成長。營益率估達 21.5%，季增 2.8/年增 5.1 個 ppts，下修 0.2 個 ppts。估
EPS 達 14.7 元，季增 36%/年增 120%，下修 1%。整體 4Q26 公司將受惠新平台 GPU/ASIC 放量，
挹注高階電源出貨及 DCDC 電源客戶加速放量，4Q26 HVDC 小量推升營運逐季成長；漲價陸續反
映將帶動 4Q26 估營收獲利同創新高。

4Q26 小量出貨 HVDC 產品，營運加速季成長 
本中心預估台達電 2026/27 年營收年增 45%/61%至 8,030/12,893 億元，下修 3%/3%。本次略調
降 AI 營收與佔比至 3,407/7,656 億元，營收占比達 42%/59%，年增 197%/125%，主要反映缺料
使產能提升不順狀況延續。本次下修 2026 年毛利率 0.2 個 ppts 至 37.0% ，上修 2027 年毛利率
0.6 個 ppts 至 40.1%；營益率估 18.9%/23.7%，年增 3.8/4.7 個 ppts，下修 0.2/上修 0.7 個 ppts。
下修主因物料成本上漲影響及物料供應不順使短期營收規模略低於預期。本中心認為 HVDC 電源
架構下，台達電將提供包含電源 /散熱相關零組件與資料中心建設解決方案，產品組合將大幅改善。
估台達電 2026/2027 年 EPS 43.1/80.8 元，年增 86%/88%，下修 1%/上修 2%。
